So... yeah, I suppose it's caused a few issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with focus and impulsivity are affecting your job performance or overall well-being?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said... yes. It's just... we're just walking around each other. It's not how his retirement is supposed to be. I don't think so. Not really. Just what I already mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or feel restless even when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: I guess... I haven't really noticed. I don't know. I haven't been doing much of anything I enjoy lately anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or have trouble focusing even when you're doing leisure activities that you normally find relaxing?\n\nPatient: Like I said... I haven't really been doing anything like that lately. So... I don't know. I guess not.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're by yourself at home?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I'm usually just sitting around when I'm home anyway. There isn't really much I need to focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're always on the go even when you're doing things you're supposed to be enjoying?\n\nPatient: I mean... maybe a little. After my shifts at work, I should be worn out. Usually I am. But lately I come home and I'm just... buzzing, I guess. Sitting on the couch just feels impossible right now. So I end up out in the garage sorting bins, or... reorganizing the kitchen cabinets. Just to be doing something. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or have trouble focusing during your daily activities at the warehouse and at home equally, or is it worse in one place than the other?\n\nPatient: I guess... probably at work. A beeper, or a forklift alarm... or even just someone moving a pallet. It pulls me right off my count. And then... well, I realize I've put the wrong numbers into the inventory.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your main problem is that you can't focus on things or that you act on impulses without thinking?\n\nPatient: I don't really know...
